CONVERTINGTEXTTOLOWERCASE

WHYIT MATTERS

Reducesvocabularysize

Lowercasingconvertsall uppercaseletterstolowercase.

Improves matching and retrieval

This ensures consistency so that different cases of the same word are treated as the same token.

Helpsmodelsgeneralisebetter

TOKENISATION:SPLITTINGTEXTINTOUNITS

WHYITMATTERS

Prepares text for analysis

Tokenisation is the process of breaking text into smaller units called tokens.

Enables counting and statistics

Tokens can be words, subwords or characters depending on the method used.

EssentialforallNLPmodels

Goal: to create meaningful, consistent units that can be processed by machines.

Improves consistency and accuracy

WHYTOKENISATIONMATTERSFORMODERNLLMS

LargeLanguageModels (LLMs)donotreadtextthewayhumansdo.

KEY TAKEAWAYS

They process text as tokens (words,subwords or pieces).The way we tokenise text has a big impact on how

modelslearn,understandandgenerate language.

Tokenisation is the foundation of how

LLMs seeand understand text.

WHAT IS STEMMING?

Stemming is a text normalization technique that reduces words to their base or root form

BESTPRACTICES

by removing prefixes and suffixes using heuristic (rule-based)methods.

It helps reducevocabularysize and treats different forms of thesame word as one.

Stemmingis language-dependent.

and consistentdatathat machinescanunderstand.

WHAT IS TEXT ENCODING?

COMMON ENCODING METHODS

Text encoding is the process of converting text (tokens or indices)

into numerical format (vectors) so that machine learning models

One-Hot Encoding

38

canprocess,learnfrom,andmakepredictions.

Each word is represented by a binary vector

with 1 at its index and 0 elsewhere.

Simple but high-dimensional.

KEYTAKEAWAYS

NOTE

One-Hot Encoding creates a binary column for each category.

Only one column has 1 for a given row; others are 0.

If your data has many categories, the number of columns (features) increases.

In such cases, consider techniques like frequencyencoding ortarget encoding.

It removes any artificial order or distance between categories.

The resulting matrix can be used directly as input to ML models.

Page 49

ADVANTAGES OF ONE-HOT ENCODING

One-Hot Encoding represents each category as a binary vector (Os and 1s).

This simple representation brings several important advantages.

1. Eliminates False

.One-hot encoding removes any artificial order or ranking between categories.

Ordinal Relationships

2. Treats All Categories

Eachcategoryisgiventhesametypeofrepresentation.

Equally

Thispreventsbiastowardcategorieswithsmallerorlargerintegervalues.

3. Works Well with Most

.Manyalgorithms (e.g.,linear models,logistic regression,neural networks)

work better with numeric inputs that are not ordinal.

Machine Learning Models

. One-hot encoding is widely compatible and model-friendly.

4. Improves Model

.Each column represents one specific category.

Interpretability

.It becomes easy to understand how much each category contributes to the prediction.

5.Simple,Safe and

. Very easy to implement.

.Introducesnomisleadinginformation.

Effective

.Provides a clean and robust representation for categorical data.

One-Hot Encoding converts categories into a binary, non-ordinal, and equal representation.

KEY TAKEAWAY

This helps machine learning models focus on real patterns, not artificial numbers.

Page 50

LIMITATIONS OF ONE-HOT ENCODING

One-Hot Encoding is simple and powerful, but it also has some important limitations.

Understanding these helps us choose the right encoding technique for the right problem.

.Creates as many columns as the number of categories.

Example:

1. High Dimensionality

If a feature has 10,000 categories,

.Leads to very wide and sparse feature matrices.

(Sparsity)

it will create 10,000 columns.

.Can increase memoryusage and computation time.

Most values will be 0.

Example:

2. Not Scalable to

.With very high-cardinality features (e.g., user_id, product_id),

Encodinguser_idofmillionsofusers

one-hotencoding becomes impractical.

High Cardinality

results inmillions of columns.

.Can lead to performance issues in training and storage.

Not scalable!

Example:

3.Ignores Relationships

.One-hot encoding treats all categories as completely independent.

It treats"Cat" and"Tiger" as unrelated,

.It cannot capture any similarity or hierarchy between categories.

Between Categories

even though they are similar.

.More features =more computations.

4. More Computational

Example:

.Increases training timeand prediction time,

Training a model with 50,000 one-hot features

Cost

especially for large datasets.

takes much longer than with fewerfeatures.

5. Not Suitable for

.Tree-based algorithms (like Decision Trees, Random Forests)

Example:

Tree-Based Models

can handle integer encoding well.

Fortree models,label/integer encoding

(Sometimes)

One-hotencodingmayaddunnecessarycomplexity.

is often sufficient and more efficient.

One-Hot Encoding is excellentfor lowto medium cardinality categorical features andworks

KEYTAKEAWAY

very well with linear models and neural networks.

For high-cardinalityfeatures, consider alternatives like Frequency Encoding,Target Encoding,

Hashing Encoding,or Embeddings.

MEANING

WHY WENEED WORDEMBEDDINGS

Traditional encodings (One-Hot, Bag-of-Words, TF-IDF, etc.) represent words as symbols or counts.

Theydonot capture meaning,contextor relationshipsbetween words.

Word embeddings solve this by representing words as dense vectors of real numbers in a continuous space.

SPARSE VS DENSE

REPRESENTATIONS

Data can be represented in many ways.Traditional methods (e.g.,One-Hot, Bag-of-Words)produce sparse vectors with mostly zeros.

Word embeddings (e.g., Word2Vec, GloVe,BERT)produce dense vectors with real-valued numbers that capture meaning and relationships.

1.SPARSEREPRESENTATIONS(Traditional)

2.DENSEREPRESENTATIONS(Modern)

High-dimensional vectors with mostly zeros.

Low-dimensional vectors with real-valued numbers.

Examples: One-Hot, Multi-Hot, Bag-of-Words, TF-IDF

Examples:Word Embeddings (Word2Vec,GloVe,FastText, BERT)

WHAT IS WORD2VEC?

Word2Vec is a familyofunsupervised learning models that learn dense vector representations

(embeddings)of wordsfrom large amounts of text by capturing the context in which words appear.

HOWWORD2VECLEARNSWORDREPRESENTATIONS

Word2Vec learns dense vector representations(embeddings)ofwordsby analyzing the context in which words appear.

It uses the idea: Words that occur in similar contexts tend to have similar meanings (Distributional Hypothesis).

TWOWORD2VECARCHITECTURES-CBOW&SKIP-GRAM

Word2Vec learns word embeddingsusing two complementaryarchitectures:

CBOW (Continuous Bag of Words) predicts a targetword from its context.

Skip-Grampredicts context words from atarget word.

Backpropagation is the algorithm that tells the neural network

We start from the output (where we see the error) and pass the error

how much each weight and bias contributed to the error (loss),

backward through the network to the earlier layers.

so we can update them to make betterpredictions.

1.FORWARD PASS (We alreadydid this)

2.BACKWARD PASS (Backpropagation)

Input

Layer

How wrong is our prediction?

(Cogr on

true answer)

We compute the loss (how wrong it is).

01

Step 2: Send the error backward

Backpropagation calculates gradients

Distribute the error to the hidden layer.

(derivatives of the loss) for every weight and bias.

Each connection gets a "blame" (gradient)

for how much it caused the error.

Using these gradients, we update the weights

so the loss becomes smaller.

Step 3: Continue backward

We repeat this over many examples (epochs)

Repeat layer by layer until we reach the input layer.

until the model learns well.

WhyStandard Neural Networks AreNot Enough

Standard (feed-forward) neural networks treat all inputs independently and do not maintain any memory of previous inputs.

This makes them unable to model the sequential nature of language, where the meaning of a word depends on the words around it.

Understanding the Hidden State

The hidden state is the memory of an RNN.It stores a compact representation of all the information seen so far

and is updated at each time step using the current input and the previous hidden state.

RNN ComputationatOneTimeStep

At each time step t, the RNN takes the current input vector xt and the previous hidden state ht-1,

computes a new hidden state ht using a non-linear transformation, and optionally produces an output yt.

From RNN to LSTM - Why Do We Need Something Better?

Standard RNNs are simpleand effective for short sequences,but they struggle to remember information overlongperiods.

LsTMs weredesignedto overcome this limitation byusing a more sophisticated memorymechanism.

Why do RNNs struggle?

Vanilla RNN:simplebut limited

LSTM:abettermemorymechanism

Main Takeaway: We need something better than vanilla RNNs because they struggle to remember information over long sequences.

LSTMs (and GRUs) address'this with gating mechanisms that preserve important information, enabling better: performance on real-world NLP tasks.

What is a GRU?

AGated RecurrentUnit(GRU)is a simpler andmore efficient variant of LsTM.It usesgating mechanismstocontrol information flow,but

with fewergatesand no separate cell state,while still addressing the vanishing gradientproblem and capturing long-term dependencies.

The GRU Cell -Basic Architecture

A GRU (Gated Recurrent Unit) uses two gates -a reset gate and an update gate -to control information flow.

Unlike an LSTM,a GRU has a single hidden state and no separate cell state, making it simpler and more efficient.

GRU cell architecture

Reset gate (rt)

Update gate (zt)

GRU cell

rt = o(W,[ht-1, xt] + b,)

The Two GRU Gates - Reset & Update

A GatedRecurrent Unit(GRU)usestwogatesto control theflow of information:

a reset gate to decide how muchpast information to forgetwhen creating new content,

and an update gate to decide how much of the new content tokeep.

The Encoder-Decoder Bottleneck

In the traditional encoder-decoder model, the entire input sequence is compressed into a single fixed-size context vector.

This creates a bottleneck, especially for long sentences, as important information from earlier words may be lost.

Traditional Encoder-Decoder (WithoutAttention)

With Attention(no bottleneck)

All source information is compressed into a single contextvector.

Thedecodercanaccessall encoderhiddenstatesandfocusonthemostrelevantones

at eachstep.

What Does an Attention Mechanism Do?

An attention mechanism learns to focus on the most relevant parts of the input sequence when generating each output word.

It computesa weighted sumofall input representations,wheretheweights(attention scores)indicatehowrelevanteach input word is to the currentword.

Step 1 - Calculate Attention Score

SIS3204Y(5)

Natural Language Processing

The attention score measures how relevant each input word is to the current output word. It is computed by comparing the decoder's current state

(query) with each encoder hidden state (key), typically using a dot product (or scaled dot product). A higher score means higher relevance.

From Attention to Self-Attention

SIS3204Y(5）

Natural Language Processing

The attention mechanism we have seen in encoder-decoder models aligns two different sequences (e:g. source and target in machine translation)

Self-attention generalises this idea by allowing each word in the same sequence to attend to all other words in that sequence.

Natural Language Processing

Self-attention allowseach word ina sequencetoattendtoall otherwordsinthesame sequence.It computes howmuch eachword shouldfocus ontheotherwords,

so that each word can build a context-aware representation that captures the meaning of the whole sentence.

Creating Q, K and V + Calculating Relevance

Each input word is first converted into an embedding vector. These embeddings are then linearly transformed into three different vectors: Query (Q),

Key (k) and Value (V).The similarity between a query and all keys is computed using a dot product to determine how relevant each word is to the current word.

Scaled Dot-Product Attention

SIS3204Y(5)

Natural Language Processing

Scaled dot-product attention computes a weighted combination of value vectors, where the weights are based on the similarity between a query and all keys.

It allows each word to attend to the most relevant words in the sequence.

What Can Self-Attention Learn?

Self-attention learns to capture different types of relationships between words by assigning higher weights to relevant words in the sentence.

It can learn local and long-range dependencies, syntactic relationships, and semantic relationships, which help build a rich contextual representation for each word.

From Single-Head to Multi-Head Attention

A single attention head can only capture one type of relationship at a time. Different types of relationships are important in a sentence

(e.g. syntactic, semantic, coreference). Multi-head attention uses multiple attention heads in parallel, so that each head can focus on a different aspect

of the sentence, and together they provide a richer representation.

What is Multi-Head Attention?

In practice,we use hattention heads

(e.g. h = 8) in parallel.

Multi-head attention performs several attention operations (heads) in parallel. Each head has its own learned projection matrices, allowing it to focus on different aspects

of the sequence. The outputs from all heads are then combined to produce the final representation.

From Self-Attention to Transformers

Self-attention is the core buildingblock of the Transformer.It allows themodel to look at all words in the sequence and build contextual representations in parallel.

ATransformercombinesself-attentionwithadditional componentssuchaspositional encoding,feed-forwardnetworks,residual connectionsandlayernormalization

to create a deep and powerful language model.

What Is Prompt Engineering?

Prompt engineering is the practice of designing and refining input prompts to obtain useful, accurate

and relevant responses from large language models.

Definition

Whyitmatters

LLMs are general-purpose models and do not have a fixed task

Prompt engineering involves crafting clear instructions,

providingrelevantcontextand specifyingthedesiredoutput
